{"actor_id":"EO:12656","char_end":44959,"char_start":43722,"confidence":"medium","effective_class":"agency_variable","effective_date":null,"effective_wording":[{"char_end":7998,"char_start":7711,"text":"(f)\n Minimum number of agents in States\nThe Attorney General shall allocate to each State not fewer than 10 full-time active duty agents of the Immigration and Naturalization Service to carry out the functions of the Service, in order to ensure the effective enforcement of this chapter."},{"char_end":8465,"char_start":7999,"text":"(g)\n Attorney General\n(1)\n In general\nThe Attorney General shall have such authorities and functions under this chapter and all other laws relating to the immigration and naturalization of aliens as were exercised by the Executive Office for Immigration Review, or by the Attorney General with respect to the Executive Office for Immigration Review, on the day before the effective date of the Immigration Reform, Accountability and Security Enhancement Act of 2002."},{"char_end":16412,"char_start":16321,"text":"Statutory Notes and Related Subsidiaries\nEffective Date of 2002 Amendment\nAmendment by Pub."},{"char_end":16623,"char_start":16413,"text":"L. 107–296 effective on the date of the transfer of functions from the Commissioner of Immigration and Naturalization to officials of the Department of Homeland Security (Mar. 1, 2003), see section 1104 of Pub."},{"char_end":16745,"char_start":16653,"text":"L. 108–7, set out as an Effective Date note under section 521 of Title 6, Domestic Security."},{"char_end":16783,"char_start":16746,"text":"Effective Date of 1996 Amendment\nPub."},{"char_end":17074,"char_start":16801,"text":"C, title I, § 134(b), Sept. 30, 1996, 110 Stat. 3009–564, provided that: “The amendment made by subsection (a) [amending this section] shall take effect 90 days after the date of the enactment of this Act [Sept. 30, 1996].”\nAmendment by section 308(d)(4)(C), (e)(4) of Pub."},{"char_end":17245,"char_start":17075,"text":"L. 104–208 effective, with certain transitional provisions, on the first day of the first month beginning more than 180 days after Sept. 30, 1996, see section 309 of Pub."},{"char_end":17360,"char_start":17310,"text":"Effective Date of 1990 Amendment\nAmendment by Pub."},{"char_end":17470,"char_start":17361,"text":"L. 101–649 effective Oct. 1, 1991, and applicable beginning with fiscal year 1992, see section 161(a) of Pub."},{"char_end":24333,"char_start":20723,"text":"E, title V, § 564(a), Dec. 26, 2007, 121 Stat. 2090, provided that:\n“(a)\n In General.—\nThe Secretary of Homeland Security shall take such actions as may be necessary to install additional physical barriers and roads (including the removal of obstacles to detection of illegal entrants) in the vicinity of the United States border to deter illegal crossings in areas of high illegal entry into the United States.\n“(b)\n Construction of Fencing and Road Improvements Along the Border.—\n“(1)\n Additional fencing along southwest border.—\n“(A)\n Reinforced fencing.—\nIn carrying out subsection (a), the Secretary of Homeland Security shall construct reinforced fencing along not less than 700 miles of the southwest border where fencing would be most practical and effective and provide for the installation of additional physical barriers, roads, lighting, cameras, and sensors to gain operational control of the southwest border.\n“(B)\n Priority areas.—\nIn carrying out this section [amending this section], the Secretary of Homeland Security shall—\n“(i)\n identify the 370 miles, or other mileage determined by the Secretary, whose authority to determine other mileage shall expire on December 31, 2008, along the southwest border where fencing would be most practical and effective in deterring smugglers and aliens attempting to gain illegal entry into the United States; and\n“(ii)\n not later than December 31, 2008, complete construction of reinforced fencing along the miles identified under clause (i).\n“(C)\n Consultation.—\n“(i)\n In general.—\nIn carrying out this section, the Secretary of Homeland Security shall consult with the Secretary of the Interior, the Secretary of Agriculture, States, local governments, Indian tribes, and property owners in the United States to minimize the impact on the environment, culture, commerce, and quality of life for the communities and residents located near the sites at which such fencing is to be constructed.\n“(ii)\n Savings provision.—\nNothing in this subparagraph may be construed to—\n     “(I)\n create or negate any right of action for a State, local government, or other person or entity affected by this subsection; or\n     “(II)\n affect the eminent domain laws of the United States or of any State.\n“(D)\n Limitation on requirements.—\nNotwithstanding subparagraph (A), nothing in this paragraph shall require the Secretary of Homeland Security to install fencing, physical barriers, roads, lighting, cameras, and sensors in a particular location along an international border of the United States, if the Secretary determines that the use or placement of such resources is not the most appropriate means to achieve and maintain operational control over the international border at such location.\n“(2)\n Prompt acquisition of necessary easements.—\nThe Attorney General, acting under the authority conferred in section 103(b) of the Immigration and Nationality Act [8 U.S.C. 1103(b)] (as inserted by subsection (d)), shall promptly acquire such easements as may be necessary to carry out this subsection and shall commence construction of fences immediately following such acquisition (or conclusion of portions thereof).\n“(3)\n Safety features.—\nThe Attorney General, while constructing the additional fencing under this subsection, shall incorporate such safety features into the design of the fence system as are necessary to ensure the well-being of border patrol agents deployed within or in near proximity to the system.\n“(4)\n Authorization of appropriations.—\nThere are authorized to be appropriated such sums as may be necessary to carry out this subsection."},{"char_end":25150,"char_start":24776,"text":"Any such decision by the Secretary shall be effective upon being published in the Federal Register.\n“(2)\n Federal court review.—\n“(A)\n In general.—\nThe district courts of the United States shall have exclusive jurisdiction to hear all causes or claims arising from any action undertaken, or any decision made, by the Secretary of Homeland Security pursuant to paragraph (1)."},{"char_end":28450,"char_start":28307,"text":"Each such report shall include an analysis of the degree to which the Attorney General’s strategy has been effective in reducing illegal entry."},{"char_end":30509,"char_start":28757,"text":"C, title III, § 371(c), Sept. 30, 1996, 110 Stat. 3009–645, provided that:\n“(1)\n In general.—\nThere shall be four levels of pay for immigration judges, under the Immigration Judge Schedule (designated as IJ–1, 2, 3, and 4, respectively), and each such judge shall be paid at one of those levels, in accordance with the provisions of this subsection.\n“(2)\n Rates of pay.—\n“(A)\n The rates of basic pay for the levels established under paragraph (1) shall be as follows:\nIJ–1\n70% of the next to highest rate of basic pay for the Senior Executive Service\nIJ–2\n80% of the next to highest rate of basic pay for the Senior Executive Service\nIJ–3\n90% of the next to highest rate of basic pay for the Senior Executive Service\nIJ–4\n92% of the next to highest rate of basic pay for the Senior Executive Service.\n“(B)\n Locality pay, where applicable, shall be calculated into the basic pay for immigration judges.\n“(3)\n Appointment.—\n“(A)\n Upon appointment, an immigration judge shall be paid at IJ–1, and shall be advanced to IJ–2 upon completion of 104 weeks of service, to IJ–3 upon completion of 104 weeks of service in the next lower rate, and to IJ–4 upon completion of 52 weeks of service in the next lower rate.\n“(B)\n Notwithstanding subparagraph (A), the Attorney General may provide for appointment of an immigration judge at an advanced rate under such circumstances as the Attorney General may determine appropriate.\n“(4)\n Transition.—\nImmigration judges serving as of the effective date shall be paid at the rate that corresponds to the amount of time, as provided under paragraph (3)(A), that they have served as an immigration judge, and in no case shall be paid less after the effective date than the rate of pay prior to the effective date.”\n[Pub."},{"char_end":30782,"char_start":30527,"text":"C, title III, § 371(d)(2), Sept. 30, 1996, 110 Stat. 3009–646, provided that: “Subsection (c) [set out above] shall take effect 90 days after the date of the enactment of this Act [Sept. 30, 1996].”\n]\nMachine-Readable Document Border Security Program\nPub."},{"char_end":32760,"char_start":31580,"text":"L. 100–690, title VII, § 7350, Nov. 18, 1988, 102 Stat. 4473, provided that:\n“(a)\n Pilot Program Regarding the Identification of Certain Aliens.—\n“(1)\n Within 6 months after the effective date of this subtitle [Nov. 18, 1988], the Attorney General shall establish, out of funds appropriated pursuant to subsection (c)(2), a pilot program in 4 cities to improve the capabilities of the Immigration and Naturalization Service (hereinafter in this section referred to as the ‘Service’) to respond to inquiries from Federal, State, and local law enforcement authorities concerning aliens who have been arrested for or convicted of, or who are the subject of any criminal investigation relating to, a violation of any law relating to controlled substances (other than an aggravated felony as defined in section 101(a)(43) of the Immigration and Nationality Act [8 U.S.C. 1101(a)(43)], as added by section 7342 of this subtitle).\n“(2)\n At the end of the 12-month period after the establishment of such pilot program, the Attorney General shall provide for an evaluation of its effectiveness, including an assessment by Federal, State, and local prosecutors and law enforcement agencies."},{"char_end":38446,"char_start":37293,"text":"Consistent with applicable law, the Task Force shall:\n(a) provide direction to executive departments and agencies (agencies) concerning the integration into American society of America’s legal immigrants, particularly through instruction in English, civics, and history;\n(b) promote public-private partnerships that will encourage businesses to offer English and civics education to workers;\n(c) identify ways to expand English and civics instruction for legal immigrants, including through faith-based, community, and other groups, and ways to promote volunteer community service; and\n(d) make recommendations to the President, through the Secretary, from time to time regarding:\n(i) actions to enhance cooperation among agencies on the integration of legal immigrants into American society;\n(ii) actions to enhance cooperation among Federal, State, and local authorities responsible for the integration of legal immigrants;\n(iii) changes in rules, regulations, or policy to improve the effective integration of legal immigrants into American society; and\n(iv) proposed legislation relating to the integration of legal immigrants into American society."},{"char_end":52039,"char_start":51779,"text":"To the extent permitted by law, the Secretary of Homeland Security may structure each agreement under section 287(g) of the INA (8 U.S.C. 1357(g)) in the manner that provides the most effective model for enforcing Federal immigration laws in that jurisdiction."}],"exceptions_text":[],"filing_date":null,"id":"ACT:EO:14012>EO:12656@b344bde70eae3335","names_target":true,"notes":"Machine lead only; no legal ending or revival asserted. and\n(b) Executive departments and agencies (agencies) shall take all appropriate action to promptly revoke all memoranda, guidance, or other policies based on the Executive Orders revoked in section 3(a) of this order and shall employ all lawful means to ensure the faithful execution of the immigration laws of the United States against all inadmissible and removable aliens.","sections_touched":["section 2","section 3(a)"],"signing_date":"1988-11-18","source":{"acceptable_as_original":false,"filing_date":null,"grade":"held_text_lead","instrument_id":"EO:12656","page_or_item":"8 U.S.C. 1103","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"f5206e4e21395303c5366e03b9ce77e2f92e4186bec4fa28a739528f0a6326cb","signing_date":"1988-11-18","text_path":"jobs/theophilus-sources/output/texts/usc-t8-s1103.txt","text_sha256":"f5206e4e21395303c5366e03b9ce77e2f92e4186bec4fa28a739528f0a6326cb","url":"https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=granuleid:USC-prelim-title8-section1103&num=0&edition=prelim"},"verb":"revoke"},{"actor_id":"EO:14148","char_end":4402,"char_start":1175,"confidence":"medium","how_named":"direct","id":"CHAIN:EO:14012#2","notes":"Change wording lead;
